# List of International Congresses of Mathematicians Plenary and Invited Speakers

::: {#o1 .subject cue=lead name="List of International Congresses of Mathematicians Plenary and Invited Speakers"}

This is a **list of International Congresses of Mathematicians Plenary and Invited Speakers**. Being invited to talk at an [International Congress of Mathematicians](wikipedia://en/627842) has been called "the equivalent, in this community, of an induction to a hall of fame."[^o2] The current list of Plenary and Invited Speakers presented here is based on the ICM's post-WWII terminology, in which the one-hour speakers in the morning sessions are called "Plenary Speakers" and the other speakers (in the afternoon sessions) whose talks are included in the ICM published proceedings are called "Invited Speakers". In the pre-WW II congresses the Plenary Speakers were called "Invited Speakers".

:::

## Overview {#Overview}

::: {#o4 .entry cue=list-entry name="Hilbert's problems"}

- During the 1900 Congress in Paris, France, David Hilbert announced his famous list of [Hilbert's problems](wikipedia://en/154584).[^o5]

:::

::: {#o6 .entry cue=list-entry name="Richard Brauer"}

- At the 1954 Congress of Mathematicians in Amsterdam, [Richard Brauer](wikipedia://en/605134) announced his program for the [classification of finite simple groups](wikipedia://en/7445).[^o7]

:::

::: {#o8 .entry cue=list-entry name="Alexander Grothendieck"}

- [Alexander Grothendieck](wikipedia://en/2042) in his plenary lecture at the 1958 Congress outlined his programme "to create arithmetic geometry via a (new) reformulation of algebraic geometry, seeking maximal generality."[^o9]

:::

::: {#o10 .entry cue=list-entry name="Kiyosi Itô"}

- At the 1962 Congress in Stockholm [Kiyosi Itô](wikipedia://en/1329240) lectured on how to combine [differential geometry](wikipedia://en/8625) and [stochastic analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stochastic_analysis), and this led to major advances in the 60s and 70s.[^o11]

:::
